# Second-order notebook

Stage 6, kept by the agent as the work happens, not afterwards. `B3 Build in self-questioning`.

One row per assumption, surprise or open item. **Kind** is *divergence* (what was assumed, what turned out to be true, what changed), *finding*, or *worklist* (the person's to do). **Status** is *done*, *open*, or *person*.

MASTER 13 000 and DEFAULT_VOLUME 50 kept from Mandible's loudness fix. | done |
| S2 | finding | The wind loop needs crossfading to loop without a click. | Running each one-pole filter round the buffer twice makes the end state carry into the start; gusts use whole cycles per second (1 and 3). | No crossfade. | A1 | finding | How the AI (PLAN P12) plans its releases. | `ai.rs`: same `Controls` as a player. On a vine it holds W toward its target and searches release moments ahead of the real swing: a copy of the ape swings on with the same controls, and from every 3rd tick a second copy flies with the same air steering and grab, recording which vine it lands on, when, with how much speed toward the vine after, and on how long a rope. One predicted flight a tick (a burst of ~20 on the grab, so the first upswing can be taken). Before letting go at the best moment it checks the landing has a way on (a second of swinging there, a release tried every 6 ticks); a dead end costs 60 m of score. Targets are ranked for a ~28 m hop on its line, penalised for hanging out of reach above. In the air it steers across its motion, lined up for the vine after, and grabs only the target or a vine beyond it. On the ground it runs at the nearest jumpable vine and jumps when a copy jumping now would catch one. Items fire at simple moments. | Deterministic physics makes the prediction exact: over full races `replans` stays 0 and predicted landings match real ones (hits 70, misses 0). | done |

Wasm is perhaps 2–4× slower: about 4% of a core for 3 AIs, with spikes up to ~10 ms. | If spikes show in the browser, spread the grab burst over 2–3 ticks (lower the `COVER / MOMENT` burst in `on_vine`). | open |

| J3 | divergence | T12: summoned vines pushed onto `race.course.vines` and removed when they vanish. | Removing would shift every later index (`Mode::Swinging { vine }`, the vine chains, the online states). The app's glue: `spectate(p, alpha)` (after each live tick: once the followed racer has been out for `SPECTATE_AFTER`, 2 s, `follow_racing` and a pan) and `refocus(p, from_camera, ticks)` (resets the chase on the new racer and starts a `camera::Pan`). T37 needs only `racing` to say "eaten" and the same `spectate` runs. | done |

When everyone is in, the full overlay as before. A/D or ←/→ switch among those still racing; live switching is only possible once the local ape is out. The camera pans (1.2 s ease in and out, `camera::Pan`) 2 s after the finish, and on to the next leader 2 s after each followed racer finishes. A Time Trial never spectates. The HUD's place, time, item and speedometer show the followed ape. | done |

Stored in the same library under `track` (an older shelf reads unchanged; FORMAT stays 1). Online runs are never a best and at most `ONLINE_KEPT` (3) are kept among the 10 recent. Not kept: held items (the HUD shows none in an online replay), thrown bananas and hooks from other browsers (they are not seen here live either), and finish times come from the places, so they can differ from the host's results by a tick or so. | done |

| VN1 | The game's version (`app::VERSION`, 1.3.0 for the Ski Slope release) shows tiny in the bottom right corner on every screen, named by the core (`ui().version`), so the page holds no number. | R55-1 | finding | TRIAGE T55: the best replay per map was kept until beaten, so it could not be lost. | Four ways lost it. (1) `Library::parse` returned an empty library for any shelf of another `FORMAT` (or any shape serde could not read), and the next finished run then saved over storage: every best gone at a format bump. (2) A best whose course fingerprint changed (any layout tuning) stopped fitting: greyed out, no ghost, and the first faster run on the new layout pushed it to the recent list, where ten later runs evicted it. (3) The page ignored a failed `localStorage` write (quota), so a new best lived only for the visit. (4) A best of another format that this game could read would have been written back without the fields it did not know. | All four closed, below. | done |
| R55-2 | decision | What "best" means. | Per map **and** mode (Race / Time Trial), as the library already was; online races are never bests. Now also per layout and format: a run only competes with the best of the same course, mode, layout and format, so a best from an older layout or format is never compared, beaten or evicted; it stays beside the new one for good. `Library::best_for(course, mode)` is the one that fits now (ghost, "Watch best"); `best_of` is the newest of this format. | `replay.rs` `Library::add`, `best_for`. Older bests grow by one per course and mode at each layout change or format bump (a 75 s run is some tens of KB at most). | done |
| R55-3 | decision | How an older best is shown. | Listed on Replays with the ★ and its time, plus "older layout" (`replays.older_layout`) or "older version" (`replays.older_version`, another `FORMAT`), and watchable: its controls replay on the course as it is now, so it may go its own way after the first changed vine. Never a ghost, never the setup's "Watch best". An online track of another layout stays disabled (its vines would not be there). | `Recording::format`, `fits` (now also checks the format), `watchable`, `older`; `app.rs` `watch`, `replays_panels`, `best_replay`. | person: look |
| R55-4 | decision | Reading another format. | Bests of any format are kept; each is written back exactly as read, with a `format` field added when its shelf named the format (`Library::foreign`), so a later or earlier build finds it whole. A best this build cannot read at all is kept the same way, unlisted. Recent runs of another format are dropped (not bests; they would replay wrongly). Shelves from before T55 (no per-recording format) read exactly as before. | `Library::parse`, `text_keeping`; `Stored.format`. | done |
| R55-5 | decision | Storage full. | `writeStore` now says whether it stored; on a failed replays write the page calls `game.replays_not_saved()`, and the core asks again next frame with one recent run fewer in the text (`Library::text_keeping`), down to the bests alone, which are never shed. The library in memory keeps everything for the visit. A failed `setItem` leaves the old text in place, so nothing already stored is wiped. | `App::replays_not_saved`, `replay_room`; `api.rs`, shim `Game::replays_not_saved`; `web/app.js` `handle`. | done |
| R55-6 | worklist | What T55 still does not cover. | (a) Watching an older best replays its controls on today's course and physics, so it shows a different run from the one timed; keeping the run itself would need a track of the apes stored with each best (the online `Track`, ~13 KB an ape for 75 s) and drawing it on a course whose vines may have moved. (b) Text in storage that is not JSON at all (truncated by hand, say) is still read as empty and saved over at the next finish. | Person: is (a) worth building? | person |
| R55-7 | finding | Rule 6 for T55 (each broken once by a script, the one test run, the file restored). | Old-format bests filtered on read → `a_best_of_another_format…` ("old-format bests dropped", left 0); foreign bests not written → `a_best_that_cannot_be_read…` (left 1 right 2); format not stamped on a foreign best → `a_best_of_another_format…` (the read-back library differs: the old best took this format); layout left out of the best's slot → `a_best_of_an_older_layout_stays…` ("the older layout's best was lost", left [Some(7000)]); `fits` ignoring the format → `a_best_of_another_format…` ("assertion failed: !b.fits(&course) && b.watchable(&course)"); `text_keeping` keeping every recent → `a_full_store_sheds…` ("the newest kept", five left); the app shedding bests when room is short → `when_storage_is_full…` ("a best was shed", left []); the app not shrinking the text → same test ("it stops"); no "older layout" note → `a_best_of_an_older_layout_is_kept…` (the panel text printed); `watch` needing an exact fit → same test ("watchable"). | | done |

| OR1 | finding | T71: one online player is never seen moving by the others, yet the race ends when they finish. | Read through `online.rs`, `online.js` and Trystero 0.25.4: a joiner's states, finishes and hits all ride one reliable ordered channel per pair, and only the round, the owner and a rising tick can reject a state, none of which differs between a state and a finish. What does differ is the path: states go straight to every peer, a finish only has to reach the host, whose results reach everyone. With STUN only (N2) two joiners can have no path to each other while both reach the host: Trystero's `onJoinError` then names the peer and the lobby status reads "Players unreachable" (it stays until the session ends), the lobby shows no ping for that seat, and in the race each sees the other stand at the start. The 1118 ms ping in the T70 screenshot fits a poor path too. Not reproduced in real browsers (no NAT here); confidence moderate. | The host passes race messages on (OR2). | done |
| OR2 | decision | How to reach a joiner with no direct link (T71). | Every `Ping` carries `near`, the client ids its browser heard from in the last 1.5 s (`NEAR_MS`, three pings; PROTOCOL 8). During a race the host passes each joiner's `State` and `Vine` on, as is, to every joiner whose `near` lacks that sender, and a `Hit` to its victim's browser. A joiner sends a hit straight to the victim only when the victim's pings say it hears this joiner, else to the host. A repeat is harmless: a state older than the newest is dropped, `place_vine` skips a summon it has, and a hit is sent one way only. A full mesh passes nothing on (test). | `online.rs` `pass_on`, `near`, `send_hit`. Tests `joiners_with_no_path_between_them_see_each_other_through_the_host`, `a_full_mesh_passes_nothing_on`. | done |
| OR3 | decision | T71's "connection problem" marker. | `Online::stalled(now)`: remote apes still racing with no state for 1.5 s (`STALL_MS`), or none since the race began. Not covered by a test (the browser check cannot close a connection under a live link). | `web/online.js` `flush`. | done |
| OR5 | decision | T72, rejoining after a refresh. | Room codes only (a pasted code is single-use). The core asks the page to keep `{room, host, id}` in the tab's `sessionStorage` (`save_online`; "" forgets) and the page hands it back as `flag.rejoin` after an invite link (which wins). The client id is reused, so the host sees the same player: a newer link with that id takes over its peer id from the old one, an old link closing is not that player leaving, and a Hello from a seat that is racing means that browser lost its race, so it is DNF in that race and gets `Underway` ("a race is under way: you join the next one"), then Rematch seats it ready. A host reopens the same code with its settings; nobody's seat is reclaimed, the joiners join again. A joiner that loses its host in the lobby retries the room for 30 s (`REJOIN_MS`, also after a refresh), then shows "Could not rejoin the room". A host who leaves on purpose still Dissolves the lobby, which forgets the room. A duplicated tab copies session storage, so two tabs could claim one client id (not handled). | `online.rs` `on_hello`, `peer_left`, `Underway`; `online_play.rs` `rejoin`, `forget_room`; `web/app.js` tab store. | done |

| SW10 | decision | SW9: how the chase camera keeps out of the Ski Slope's conifers. | Cause: the chase camera (`camera.rs`) had no obstacle avoidance on any map, only a floor over the ground; 10–14 m behind the ape, it went into the big fence conifers (radius up to 8 m) and the forest whenever the ape swung near the fence (over AI races: 60–250 ticks a race inside a tree). A "reach" glides in (accel-limited, 6 cm/tick², at most 40 cm/tick) ahead of a tree with a 3 m berth, also looking 0.3 s ahead, and glides back out slowly (1 cm/tick²); a hard clamp keeps the eye 60 cm out of any tree it was not already in. Not avoided: a tree the ape is inside or within 2 m of (the eye could only clear it by touching the ape); one the ape drew the eye into, it glides out of. A tree between the eye and the ape (occlusion) is left be. Race physics untouched. | Test `on_the_ski_slope_the_chase_camera_keeps_out_of_the_conifers_smoothly` (`app/ski_camera_tests.rs`, two AI races). Rule 6: hard clamp off gives "seed 1: the eye went into a tree at ticks [3577]". Left: 9–12 quick moves (over 30 cm change in step) a race where a tree comes faster than the glide; the worst about 5 m in a tick (the eye on the far side of a tree that sweeps over it jumps in front of it). Before: none over 14 cm, but the eye sat in needles. | done |

Cause: a pit's spikes flung straight up (14 m/s) and halved the speed along, so the ape landed back on the spikes again and again; air steering cannot speed it up (`fly`: never faster), and the pit vines (±7 m across) were out of reach from most of the pit; only the door shutting 8 s after opening let it out, and that same tick the pit vines went up (instantly, while the door's leaves still took 0.5 s to swing shut) - the vines vanishing under the person, and from the hand of anyone swinging on them. Fix (`ruins/rules.rs`, `ruins.rs`): (1) a pit's spikes throw the ape forward along the course over the pit's far edge (`PIT_THROW` 1.1 s to pass it, 2 m past; at least half its own forward speed; at most ~12.7 m/s from the pit's near end, about a run: a penalty of ~1.6 s and the swing's speed, not a boost); floor spike traps unchanged; (2) `Race::hold_doors`: while an ape is down in an open pit or holds one of its vines, the door stays open (and its vines down) at least `TRAP_HOLD` (1 s) more, by moving `tripped_at` on (race state: synced and kept in save points like the wire; run for remote apes from their reported state). After: 1.60 s worst with no keys, 1.59 s with W + grab (the AI the same: the throw needs no input). Drawing and physics already used the one `vine_up`; the new test `a_door_never_shuts_on_an_ape_in_its_pit_and_its_vines_stay_drawn` checks the frame's vines batch has the pit vines while open (270 → more shapes), keeps them as long as an ape is in the pit past 8 s, and drops them after. Not seen on screen (no screenshot): if the vines still vanish while the door is open, the cause is elsewhere (page). Rule 6: throw off → "out of a pit after 800 cs with no keys, 800 cs with W + grab (worst pit 0, -5 m along, -14 m across)"; hold off → "shut on the ape at tick 5". Both restored. `ruins_ai` (release, 8 layouts) unchanged to the tenth: Sloth 89.7 / Monkey 78.8 / Ape 72.4 / King Kong 64.9 s; the time trial too (Author 0:56); the difficulty report's Sloth mean `inf` (a layout not finished) is there before the change too. | `ruins.rs`, `ruins/rules.rs`, `race.rs` | done |

Fixes, all in the layout and rules: (1) the river's middle lane always has a vine (a station of two vines by the banks left an ape swinging bank to bank) and vines sit ±2 m along, not ±3; (2) the pin of the river's last station 14 m short of the bridge made the hop before it 29–34 m (the stall at ~510–530 m): a station halfway now comes first, so no hop is longer than 28 m; (3) the bridge's deck 34 m over the lip (was 28) and its boosted vines 24 m long (were 18): an ape swinging off the river's last vines passed over the old vines' tops, whirling 2–3 s to find a release that dropped onto them; (4) the chamber's invisible inner wall opens 24 m short of the turn in (`INNER_OPEN`): apes on the turn's first vines slammed into it for seconds. Tried and dropped: a gentler boost (`BOOST_DECENT` 2 m/s, no change), shorter vines mid-bend (none), the AI counting a boosted vine as always having a way on (none: dead ends were not the cause). Only vine positions, the deck height and a wall moved; the contract types in `ruins.rs` are unchanged. `examples/ruins_vines.rs` lists every vine; `ruins_trace` prints the target and candidates with `RUINS_TRACE_CANDS=1`. | `ruins/layout.rs`, `ruins/rules.rs` | done |

The Ape's worst layout 85.3 s (was 89.5), its three 80–90 s layouts down to two. Exact fixes: `collide` skips `support_at` on Ruins when the ape is in the air more than `FLOOR_OVER_LAND` (8 m) over the land and over every platform near (`Course::ruins_clear`; test `clear_of_everything_means_nothing_holds_an_ape` checks the floor is never higher on 4 layouts; with the margin at −2 m it fails: "layout 0: -20 m along, -32 m across, 0 m up"); `water_at` tests "off the water" by squares (`longer_than`); `Slab::contains` does its cheap tests before `right_of`. `ruins_report -- 16` identical before and after; Jungle, City, Ski and Sector untouched. That think 1.21 ms (best of 40) after; water_at 3% of it. `frame_cost -- ruins` (load ~18 on the machine): 600 frames worst 1.6 ms (frame 389), median 46 µs, none over 2 ms; 2000 frames worst 2.75 ms (frame 855, was 4.0), 99th 1.5 ms (was 2.1), 4–6 frames over 2 ms (was 21–29). | BB1 | decision | Banana Bay Battle skeleton: the contract. | `bay.rs`: `BayLayout` on `Course.bay` (`None` on the other five maps; every new rule and drawing is gated on it). Fields: `ships` (`Ship`: `kind` Galleon/Frigate/Brig/Sloop, `side` Pirate/Navy, `at` waterline middle, `yaw`, `length`, `beam`, `deck` height, `raised` decks (from, to, height), `bowsprit`, `masts` (`Mast`: `along`, `height`, `yards` (`Yard`: `height`, `half_span`, `sail`), `gaff`), `jib`, `ports` (`Port`: `along`, `side` ±1, `up`), `decks` = indices in `Course.platforms`); `Sail` Set/Furled/Torn/Missing with `Sail::clear` (only `Set` blocks a swing); `hangers` (one per vine: `Yard{ship,mast,yard,out}`, `Mast{ship,mast,up}`, `Bowsprit{ship,out}`, `Shore{foot}`; `Hanger::point`), `boundary` (`BoundaryBarrel`: `at`, `edge` ±1), `bouncy` (`BouncyBarrel`: `bouncer` index in `Course.mushrooms`, `yaw`; drawing may bob it up to `BOB_MAX`), `water` (`Water`: `level`, `waves` (`Wave`: amp, dir, length, period); `Water::height(x, z, tick)` up-and-down only), `dock` (`Dock`: from, to, half width, start `platform`), `island` (`Island`: centre, radius, `chest`, `chest_yaw`, `chest_size`), `volleys` (`Volley`: from ship+port, `at` ship, `target`, `hit`, `fire`, `flight`; repeats every `VOLLEY_CYCLE` = 20 s; `BayLayout::ball_at(i, tick)` a parabola `ARC` = 4 m high), `fighters` (`Fighter`: ship, `at`, yaw, side, `foe`, `phase`), `lobby`. Helpers: `Ship::point/fwd/right/mast_top/yard_ends/over`, `BayLayout::ship_under`. Starboard is `geom::right_of(fwd)`. Rules named in the module doc for the course agent: water reset to the last ship an ape was over (Floor is Lava: the water is the floor), barrel soft wall via `Course::contain`, bouncy barrels as plain mushrooms. | `bay/tests.rs` (5 tests). Rule 6, broken once each: swell ignoring the tick → "it moves: 81876..81876"; `Sail::clear` always true → "assertion failed: !Sail::Set.clear()"; `ball_at` never landing → "not fired yet" (left/right); `ship_under` always ship 0 → `left == right` failed at the ship-under check. | done |

| BC1 | decision | Banana Bay Battle course: the first real layout (`bay/layout.rs`). | Dock 0 m (a pier from 40 m behind the start to 14 m past it, 18 m wide, `DOCK_TOP` 12 m over the water; the start row's seven 29 m ropes hang from a gantry 30 m over its end, `Hanger::Shore` with the gantry's two posts at ±12 m as feet). Then `SHIPS` = 30 still ships bow to stern along the course (8 galleons 52 m, 8 frigates 44 m, 8 brigs 32 m, 6 sloops 26 m; two big then two small, "small ships fill the gaps"), pirates 5 m left of the line and navy 5 m right, alternating, so neighbours duel broadside to broadside; hulls at least 3 m apart along. Stations are the roped masts (galleon/frigate fore and mizzen, both brig masts, the sloop's mast; the big ships' mainmasts carry none): two or three ropes from the swing yard (second-highest of four, highest of three) at lanes −9/0/+9 m (±1) across the course, never within 3 m of the mast, hops 23–28 m between ships; plus one low rope per ship from the foremast's lower yard over the deck, 3 m above it. The island: a beach platform (2 m) from 4 m past the last bow to 160 m past the finish, a row of three palm ropes (`Hanger::Shore`) a hop past the last ship, the finish 22 m on, the chest 28 m past it. Length 1,458 m, 158 ropes. Water level 0 (`Terrain::Flat(0)`, so `Course::floor` is the still water), three swells (22, 12, 6 cm). Crates every 240 m (item ropes as elsewhere), banana arcs as the Jungle. Boundary barrels every 10 m on both edges at `half_width` (20 m); one bouncy barrel per ship on its open side (top 1.3 m, radius 2.2 m); 2–3 shots each way per neighbouring pair (145 volleys); 1–3 duelling pairs of fighters per deck (140). Lobby: the dock's landward end, 28 m behind the start, facing the fleet. New placement draws from `Dice(seed ^ 0xBA7_5EA)`. Tools: `examples/bay_layout.rs`, `bay_trace.rs`, `bay_report.rs`. | `bay/layout.rs` | done |

Lightning from the frame tick alone (`lightning`): a strike in three of four 7 s windows, flickering bright/dim/bright then fading over a third of a second; it whitens the sky, fog and light (`flashed`, in `App::frame`) and draws a jagged bolt 420 m off with a sky glow. | `bayscape.rs`, `snow.rs`, `app.rs`. Shots `notes/bay-shots/sky/squall-*.png`. | done |
| BS4 | decision | The sea surface. | Drawn from `App::frame` (every 3D stage but the lineup: race, title flyover, setup preview) by `bayscape::dynamic`, at the race's tick in a race (the drawn sea is where the physics has it) and the frame tick elsewhere. Square bands round the eye, world-grid fixed: 4 m tiles to 40 m, 8 m to 96 m, 16 m to 208 m, 32 m to 416 m (a squall's fog hides the last); each a thin slab (30 cm, 70 cm in a squall) sheared to the swell's slope at its middle, so a step between neighbours shows water; outer bands leave out swells shorter than two tiles and sit 3 cm lower each where they overlap; tiles more than ~100° off the view are skipped. A flat sheet under it all. Colour: deep (16, 84, 148) to clear (24, 122, 174) by slow patches, turquoise (50, 196, 192) within 60 m of the island's beach and 14–64 m of the dock, lighter on crests; foam flecks on crests and twinkling glints on the two near bands. | `bayscape::sea`. | done |

| BT1 | finding | Why the Sloth was no slower than the Monkey on the Bay, and the fix. | Not the water or the deck rule: over 16 layouts (`bay_trace`) the Monkey never falls in the water, and the Sloth (as tuned now) once, King Kong once. With the Jungle's handicaps (16 layouts: Sloth 72.7, Monkey 67.1, Ape 56.3, King Kong 49.7 s; some Sloth runs 56 s) the ships' ropes swing fast and long, so the Monkey's longer reaction (18 ticks to the Sloth's 14) lets the good release go by about as often as the Sloth's weaker arms cost it. Measured one knob at a time: Sloth reaction 18 → 76.8 s; Sloth arms 1 of 8 → 83.0 s; both → 80.3 s, but with arms 1 of 8 the Sloth put back on ships 26 and 27 did not get off within 12 s (`every_level_gets_off_every_deck…`: "sloth on ship 26: at 1256 m, the next stern at 1283 m"), so its arms stay 2 of 8. The Ape at 4 ticks was 6.6 s off King Kong: 8 ticks → 59.7 s. The Ape's misjudging is touchy here: 3 m → 60.8 s; none → 58.5 s but one layout (12) left it whirling over the top of a rope for about 50 s (107 s finish, 9 rescues; the Monkey shows the same whirl for ~8 s at 1,262–1,289 m on layout 2), so it stays 2 m. Now `ai::BAY_*` (Bay only, `On.bay`): reaction [18, 18, 8, 0], jitter [14, 14, 4, 0], arms [2, 3, 4, 8], misjudging [6, 5, 2, 0] m. 16 layouts: Sloth 76.8, Monkey 67.1, Ape 59.7, King Kong 49.7 s (gaps 9.7, 7.4, 10.0). Worst single run 105.3 s (Sloth, layout 11). New test `bay_difficulties_are_ordered_and_spaced` (16 layouts, `GAP` 6 s). Break once (the Jungle's Sloth handicaps back) → "sloth (72.7 s) is not 6 s slower than monkey (67.1 s)"; restored, passing. | `ai.rs`, `tests/bay_ai.rs` | done |

Author just under the best kind, then the Jungle's steps of 7, 10 and 15 s: Author 0:35.00, Gold 0:42.00, Silver 0:52.00, Bronze 1:07.00 (`trial::BAY_*`). Every hull, bowsprit included, ≥ 60 m from the race line (`NEAREST`; the barrels are at 20 m) and ≤ 250 m (`FARTHEST`), ≥ 30 m past the course's ships, 80 m from the pier, 50 m from the island. 51 ships on the course, 36 in duels; 1 in 3 not burning has every sail set; 1 in 5 burns, listing 5–11°. Still: `baymodels::ship_far` (about 30 shapes against `ship`'s 300+). Each frame within 380 m: every 8.5 s each duelling ship fires a 3-gun broadside (`smoke_far`: a flash and 2 puffs, against `smoke_puff`'s 12 + halos), the ball arcs over 70 ticks, two in three hit (`hull_hit`), the rest splash short; fires flicker and a smoke column rises 30 m. `battle::cached` keeps the placement per course (thread-local) so it is not redone each frame. | `bayscape/battle.rs`, `bayscape/fleet.rs`, `baymodels.rs` | done |

| SH1 | finding | T88: the wreck's hidden passage, measured before any change (`sector_report -- 32`, now with a 1100–1360 m split for the wreck by way taken; Chimpanzee, Equal Stats). | The passage was not slower than going round when threaded cleanly, but no faster either: King Kong through 4.6–5.4 s (mean 5.1, 4 of 32 runs), round 5.0–24 s (median ~8.7 s, the best clean ones 5.0–5.9 s). It came out at the speed it went in (~52 m/s, seed 0). Why it felt worse: the 10 × 7 m hole is 8.4 × 5.4 m for the ape's 0.8 m hit ball (`HIT_RADIUS`), and it is 34 m deep plus 39 m from the snipe vine: at 30 m/s low gravity (9 m/s²) drops you ~9 m over that run, so anything but a release dead level at speed clips the hull, which is not bouncy (a dead stop). Only King Kong aims for it (4 of 32); the others threaded it by luck (Sloth 5, Monkey 2, Ape 3). | | done |
| SH2 | decision | T88: how the shortcut pays and reads. | — | (1) The gap 10 × 7 → 14 × 10 m (`spacecraft::WRECK_GAP`, also the gallery's wreck; the hull allows 17 × 18). (2) The launch (`Ape::passage_launch`, `sector::PASSAGE_LAUNCH` 20 m/s, `PASSAGE_FLOOR` 60 m/s): flying into the opening on the way on, the ape is turned straight down the passage at its speed + 20 m/s (at least 60, at most `SECTOR_TOP` 85), once on the way in, so once in it threads itself; going in backwards does nothing. It is in the ape's physics, so the AI's copies plan with it. (3) A new `Happening::Launched` → `Reward::Shortcut`: the HUD flashes "Shortcut boost!" (`hud.reward.shortcut`) with the Speed Boost sound. (4) Five glowing mint launch rings (`LAUNCH` (60,255,200)) frame the opening mouth to mouth, lit in turn toward the exit, so the hole is visible and says "boost". `Wreck` now carries its `Passage` (worked out once), and `Passage` its `across` and `up`. No guide vine added: the snipe vine already sits on the line, and the bigger hole and the straightening do the work. Not looked at in a browser. | done |
